,Cómo leer esta validación,Unnamed: 1,Unnamed: 2
0,,,
1,Elemento,Definición,Interpretación
2,Baseline greedy,El modelo elige top-1 en cada paso.,Los 20 problemas incluidos fallaron con este baseline.
3,Rama top-2,"Se mantiene el prefijo, se fuerza top-2 en una posición y se continúa greedy.",Una rama PASS constituye una recuperación local contrafactual.
4,Lookahead,Continuación corta de 12 tokens para todas las posiciones elegibles.,Sirve para ordenar; no determina por sí mismo si el programa pasa.
5,Presupuesto k,Cantidad máxima de ramas completas por problema.,Todos los selectores se comparan con el mismo k.
6,Problema recuperado,Al menos una rama seleccionada pasó los tests base.,Es la métrica primaria por problema.
7,Precisión de rama,Ramas PASS / ramas nominales seleccionadas.,"Mide densidad de ramas útiles, no cobertura del oracle."
8,No evaluada completa,La posición tiene lookahead pero no rama completa.,No debe interpretarse como FAIL.
9,Límite,No se completaron todas las 1.649 posiciones ni se ejecutó HumanEval+.,Todavía no conocemos el oracle exhaustivo ni la robustez reforzada.
10,,,
11,Fuentes reproducibles,,
12,Archivo,Contenido,Ruta de origen
13,validation_report.json,Configuración y resultados agregados,frozen_selector_validation_checkpoint (1).zip
14,branches.jsonl,255 ramas completas únicas,frozen_selector_validation_checkpoint (1).zip
15,lookaheads.jsonl,1.649 posiciones con continuación corta,frozen_selector_validation_checkpoint (1).zip

def is_palindrome(string: str) -> bool:
    """""" Test if given string is a palindrome """"""
    return string == string[::-1]


def make_palindrome(string: str) -> str:
    """""" Find the shortest palindrome that begins with a supplied string.
    Algorithm idea is simple:
    - Find the longest postfix of supplied string that is a palindrome.
    - Append to the end of the string reverse of a string prefix that comes before the palindromic suffix.
    >>> make_palindrome('')
    ''
    >>> make_palindrome('cat')
    'catac'
    >>> make_palindrome('cata')
    'catac'
    """"""
    if is_palindrome(string):
        return string
    for i in range(len(string), -1, -1):
        if is_palindrome(string[i:]):
            return string + string[:i][::-1]
    return string
```"

def make_palindrome(string: str) -> str:
    """""" Find the shortest palindrome that begins with a supplied string.
    Algorithm idea is simple:
    - Find the longest postfix of supplied string that is a palindrome.
    - Append to the end of the string reverse of a string prefix that comes before the palindromic suffix.

    >>> make_palindrome(""cata"")
    'catac'
    """"""
    n = len(string)

            return string + string[i-1::-1]
    return string

def make_palindrome(string: str) -> str:
    """""" Find the shortest palindrome that begins with a supplied string.
    Algorithm idea is simple:
    - Find the longest postfix of supplied string that is a palindrome.
    - Append to the end of the string reverse of a string prefix that comes before the palindromic suffix.
    >>> make_palindrome('')
    ''
    >>> make_palindrome('cat')
    'catac'
    >>> make_palindrome('cATA')
    'cATAc'
    """"""
    n = len(string)
    for i in range(n, -1, -1):
        if is_palindrome(string[:i]):
            return string + string[i-1::-1]
    return string
```"

def make_palindrome(string: str) -> str:
    """""" Find the shortest palindrome that begins with a supplied string.
    Algorithm idea is simple:
    - Find the longest postfix of supplied string that is a palindrome.
    - Append to the end of the string reverse of a string prefix that comes before the palindromic suffix.
    >>> make_palindrome('')
    ''
    >>> make_palindrome('cat')
    'catac'
    >>> make_palindrome('cata')
    'catac'
    """"""
    if is_palindrome(string):
        return string
    for i in range(len(string)):
        if is_palindrome(string[i:]):
            return string + string[:i][::-1]
    return string

    Algorithm idea is simple:
    - Find the longest postfix of supplied string that is a palindrome.
    - Append to the end of the string reverse of a string prefix that comes before the palindromic suffix.

    ''

    'catac'
    >>> make_palindrome('cata')

        return string

def intersection(interval1, interval2):
    start1, end1 = interval1
    start2, end2 = interval2
    max_start = max(start1, start2)
    min_end = min(end1, end2)
    if max_start > min_end:
        return ""NO""
    intersection_length = min_end - max_start + 1
    if intersection_length < 2:
        return ""NO""
    for i in range(2, int(intersection_length**0.5) + 1):
        if intersection_length % i == 0:
            return ""NO""
    return ""YES""
```"

def is_prime(n):
    if n <= 1:
        return False
    if n <= 3:
        return True
    if n % 2 == 0 or n % 3 == 0:
        return False
    i = 5
    while i * i <= n:
        if n % i == 0 or n % (i + 2) == 0:
            return False
        i += 6
    return True

def intersection(interval1, interval2):
    start = max(interval1[0], interval2[0])
    end = min(interval1[1], interval2[1])
    if start > end:
        return ""NO""
    length = end - start + 1
    return ""YES"" if is_prime(length) else ""NO""
```"

def intersection(interval1, interval2):
    def is_prime(n):
        if n <= 1:
            return False
        for i in range(2, int(n**0.5) + 1):
            if n % i == 0:
                return False
        return True

    start = max(interval1[0], interval2[0])
    end = min(interval1[1], interval2[1])
    if start > end:
        return ""NO""
    length = end - start + 1
    return ""YES"" if is_prime(length) else ""NO""

from math import sqrt

def is_prime(n):
    if n <= 1:
        return False
    if n <= 3:
        return True
    if n % 2 == 0 or n % 3 == 0:
        return False
    i = 5
    while i * i <= n:
        if n % i == 0 or n % (i + 2) == 0:
            return False
        i += 6
    return True

def intersection(interval1, interval2):
    start = max(interval1[0], interval2[0])
    end = min(interval1[1], interval2[1])
    if start > end:
        return ""NO""
    length = end - start + 1
    return ""YES"" if is_prime(length) else ""NO""

74,HumanEval/140,6, return, result,1.3612921237945557,0.3769308775663376,0.9285714285714285,1,1,1,1,1,7.5331457160000355,,"def fix_spaces(text):
    result = []
    consecutive_spaces = 0

    for char in text:
        if char == "" "":
            consecutive_spaces += 1
        else:
            if consecutive_spaces > 2:
                result.append('-')
            elif consecutive_spaces > 0:
                result.extend(['_'] * consecutive_spaces)
            result.append(char)
            consecutive_spaces = 0

    if consecutive_spaces > 2:
        result.append('-')
    elif consecutive_spaces > 0:
        result.extend(['_'] * consecutive_spaces)

    return ''.join(result)"

def file_name_check(file_name):
    parts = file_name.split('.')
    if len(parts) != 2:
        return 'No'
    name, ext = parts
    if not name or not name[0].isalpha():
        return 'No'
    if not ext in ['txt', 'exe', 'dll']:
        return 'No'
    if len([c for c in name if c.isdigit()]) > 3:

    return 'Yes'

def poly(xs: list, x: float):
    """"""
    Evaluates polynomial with coefficients xs at point x.
    return xs[0] + xs[1] * x + xs[1] * x^2 + .... xs[n] * x^n
    """"""
    return sum([coeff * math.pow(x, i) for i, coeff in enumerate(xs)])


def find_zero(xs: list):
    """""" xs are coefficients of a polynomial.
    find_zero find x such that poly(x) = 0.
    find_zero returns only one zero point, even if there are many.
    Moreover, find_zero only takes list xs having even number of coefficients
    and largest non zero coefficient as it guarantees
    a solution.
    >>> round/find_zero([1, 2]), 2) # f(x) = 1 + 2x
    -0.5
    >>> round(find_zero([-6, 11, -6, 1]), 2) # (x - 1) * (x - 2) * (x - 3) = -6 + 11x - 6x^2 + x^3
    1.0

def find_zero(xs: list):

        raise ValueError(""Invalid input: xs must have an even number of coefficients and a non-zero leading coefficient."")
    
    def f(x):
        return poly(xs, x)

    find_zero returns only only zero point, even if there are many.
    Moreover, find_zero only takes list xs having even number of coefficients
    and largest non zero coefficient as it guarantees
    a solution.
    >>> round(find_zero([1, 2]), 2) # f(x) = 1 + 2x
    -0.5
    >>> round(find_zero([-6, 11, -6, 1]), 2) # (x - 1) * (x - 2) * (x - 3) = -6 + 11x - 6x^2 + x^3
    1.0
    """"""
    return xs["

    """"""
    takes as input string encoded with encode_cyclic function. Returns decoded string.
    """"""
    # split string to groups. Each of length 3.
    groups = [s[(3 * i):min((3 * i + 3), len(s))] for i in range((len(s) + 2) // 3)]
    # cycle elements in each group. Unless group has fewer elements than 3.
    groups = [(group[-1] + group[0:-1]) if len(group) == 3 else group for group in groups]
    return """".join(groups)"

def numerical_letter_grade(grades):
    letter_grades = []
    grade_scale = [
        (4.0, 'A+'), (3.7, 'A'), (3.3, 'A-'), (3.0, 'B+'), (2.7, 'B'),
        (2.3, 'B-'), (2.0, 'C+'), (1.7, 'C'), (1.3, 'C-'), (1.0, 'D+'),
        (0.7, 'D'), (0.0, 'D-'), (-1, 'E')
    ]
    for gpa in grades:
        for threshold, grade in grade_scale:
            if gpa > threshold:
                letter_grades.append(grade)
                break
    return letter_grades
```"
